# HistLog — The Historical and Open Ledger of the Qubitick Corpus

**Role.** The single ledger file of the canon. §0 records provenance and the restructure. §I is the declaration history: the former declared-choice registry (P-H0–P-H20a) in full, with the disposition ledger mapping every entry to its locus in the era canon, and the revision registries. §II is the open ledger: the Open Gaps Log (items 1–21, historical numbering preserved), the reframed question ledger Q1–Q12, and the declaration-requirements list. §III is the status ledger: the per-tag status lines collected from the canon at the era restructure. §IV is the adjudication record: the closure-tier tags and the tier ledger updates, preserved as history. §V is the verification history.
**Citation discipline.** Canon tags cite this file row-wise (“HistLog §I/P-H7”, “Open Gaps 7”, “item 21(i)”, “Q10”). These citations are pointers and scope markers, never premises: no theorem's truth depends on a row of this file. The canonical declaration surface of the corpus is the interface definitions Def-H.35–Def-H.43, the constitutive clauses of Def-H.3/4/8/16/19/24/26/31/32/34, and the era calibration registries Cal-2/Cal-F/Cal-BM.

---

## §0 — Provenance and the restructure record

**(0.1) Source lineage.** The corpus derives from the symbolic axiom set `A_Qubitick_axiomset.md` (adopted at the axioms; symbol reconciliations: Part 1 §0.5). The first-edition fermionic-layer files `H_archive/H01–H21` (retained in this tree as the historical locus) were consolidated into the letter series H–T under a byte-identity consolidation law; the letter series and its law are superseded by the era restructure recorded here.

**(0.2) The era restructure.** The canon was converted from the letter-series layout (A–G frozen corpus; H, J, K, L, M, N, O, P, Q, R working parts; S registries; T closure tier) into the four-era chain:
$$
\textbf{Part 1 · Qubit\_era}\ (\text{A axioms/conventions/qubit-era definitions};\ \text{B foundations})\ \to\ \textbf{Part 2 · Bosonic\_era}\ (\text{A definitions D2–D4};\ \text{C geometry};\ \text{D electroweak};\ \text{E electromagnetic};\ \text{F colour};\ \text{G saturation})
$$
$$
\to\ \textbf{Part 3 · Fermion\_era}\ (\text{the structural tags of H, J, K, L, M, N, O, P, Q, R};\ \text{the interface layer Def-H.35–43},\ \text{FT.153–155})\ \to\ \textbf{Part 4 · Baryomesonic\_era}\ (\text{Def-BM.*, BM.*}),
$$
with the two annexes: **Census.md** (the 25 Category-B cells, separated from the theorems by the charter rule) and this file. Conversion rules applied to the transplanted texts: (i) **Status.** paragraphs extracted to §III; (ii) every registry token (P-H*) replaced by its disposition locator per §I's disposition ledger; (iii) letter-part locators (A–G, H01–H21, parts S/T) replaced by era locators; (iv) the adjudication-summary tags (FT.115, FT.121, FT.132, FT.140) and the closure tier (FT.146–FT.152) moved to §IV; (v) theorem mathematics (Thm/Req/Pf/Phys blocks) transplanted unmodified except for the recorded replacements; (vi) the two scope-carrying status paragraphs of the frozen corpus (CT1.19b, EM.24) were promoted in place to **Rmk (scope)** blocks — every other status line was extracted to §III; (vii) era-law markers were made explicit on the three re-verification forward citations of the frozen corpus (CT0.14 $\to$ CT3.8; CT1.11a $\to$ CT2.8(1), EM.9; CT1.11b $\to$ EW.32 was already marked; CT0.13's $\to$ CT3.4/CT3.5 citation is a 'cf.' corroboration note) — no derivation text was altered. Tag identifiers (CT*, EW*, EM*, FT.*, Def-H.*, Def-EM.*, L*) are corpus-wide and were preserved.

**(0.3) The declaration pass (Step 1).** Every entry of the former declared-choice registry was dispositioned before the conversion: derived entries cite their deriving tags; definition-class entries were restructured as constitutive clauses of definitions or as the interface definitions Def-H.35–Def-H.43; entries that were discharged, reduced, retired, or adjudicated vacuous are recorded in §I with their anchors. The disposition ledger (§I.3) is the complete map; no entry was silently dropped.

**(0.4) Integrity findings at the restructure.** (a) The byte-identity law of the consolidation had one exception in the received tree: the first-edition FT.6 **Thm** line reads `$ g_{\mu\nu}\mapsto\Omega'^2g_{\mu\nu}...` (space after the opening `$`) in `H_archive/H01_flux_and_fermion.md` while the consolidated text reads `$g_{\mu\nu}\mapsto...` — a one-character whitespace drift; the consolidation checker (`verify_H_rung_closure.py` §A) failed on it in the received tree (suite state: 56/57). The era canon carries the consolidated reading; the byte-identity law is retired by (0.2) and the drift is recorded here. (b) The fixed-phase scope tokens (“Comm-min”, “Eff”) surviving in the fermionic-layer texts are historical: both premises were dissolved at corpus level (§I.4, Dissolution Registry (1)–(2)); the translation rule is Part 3 §0.2(ii). (c) No other divergence between the archive and the consolidated texts was found (machine scan of all 21 first editions against their letter parts at the restructure). (d) Two LaTeX double-superscript typos in the frozen canon (CT1.18's gauge-fixing block: `$\\hat h^{(4)}^\\lambda{}\\dots$`, seven occurrences, and its `$\\hat h^{(4)}^{\\lambda\\rho}$` inverse-metric line) are illegal in LaTeX and were repaired at the transplant by merging the superscript groups (`$\\hat h^{(4)\\lambda}{}\\dots$`, `$\\hat h^{(4)\\lambda\\rho}$`); the mathematical content is unchanged, and the legacy files keep the original spelling; likewise Def-H.32(6)'s `$\\text{n_V}$` (a subscript inside `\\text`, illegal in LaTeX though tolerated by browser renderers) reads `$\\mathrm{n}_\\mathrm{V}$` in the era canon; and FT.105(5)'s covering-census array carried five end-of-row `$\&\&$` cells against a four-column preamble (an extra empty cell per row — illegal in LaTeX, tolerated by browser renderers): the trailing empty cell is dropped in the era canon. (e) Three array-syntax defect classes, found at the first full-engine render pass after the Tier BM.24 integration, were repaired at the source; the mathematical content is unchanged throughout (row ends and rules only). (i) The Part 4 closure-export table's BM.144-group final row lacked its row end `\\` before the `\hline` that BM.24 appended — the fatal `! Misplaced \noalign` that halted the engine at line 113724 of the generated paper. (ii) FT.159(2)'s adjudication table (Part 3, Tier F.44) carried doubled backslashes: its row ends were written `\\\\` against the intended `\\`, and its rule was written `\\hline` against the intended `\hline` — the latter tokenizes as a row break plus the literal text 'hline' opening the first data cell, the former scattered empty rows. (iii) 347 inflated backslash runs (adjacent lengths 4, 5 and 9) across 61 single-line arrays of Part 4 (Tiers BM.13–BM.22) — extra row breaks rendering as empty rows between the table rows — were collapsed onto the corpus's own healthy idiom: even runs to the single row end, odd runs to the row end plus the command's own backslash. All three classes are now rejected at the source by `check_format.py` (a new array-syntax scan beside the existing math-segment checks) and by the `render_paper.py` pre-flight (each fault reported with the nearest preceding heading); the Summary of Record and the FT.158 derivation trace are regenerated.

## §I — Declaration history and disposition ledger

**§I.1 The former declared-choice registry (canonical historical text).** The entries below are the registry as it stood in the letter-series canon (part S, first edition `H_archive/H13_registries_and_gaps.md`). They are historical records: every entry has been dispositioned by §I.3 into the era canon (as a definition, a derived theorem, an interface definition, a calibration entry, or a retirement), and the registry tokens (P-H*) occur in the canon nowhere. The verdict column of the letter-series audit is preserved at §IV/FT.147.

**Declared-Choice Registry (non-binding, logged).**
**(P-H0)** Def-H.4 working hypothesis: fermion $=$ terminus of a Janus-closed bound chain — the identifying content of this document.
**(P-H1)** Def-H.3 modeling choice: extractable information $\equiv$ transit entropy carried ($I\equiv\mathsf E_{\mathrm{tr}}$ carried).
**(P-H2)** FT.1/FT.3/FT.4 sup-attainment: saturating transit configurations exist. **[DISCHARGED: witnesses exhibited — FT.36(2)'s photon schedules; entry retained as ledger history.]**

**(P-H4)** FT.17 hypothesis 2: per-vertex caps do not further reduce channel-visible content.
**(P-H5)** Def-H.16 boundary-state read: $|\mathrm{in}\rangle,|\mathrm{out}\rangle$ of $Z(\mathcal R,\Sigma)$ are boundary-channel states via $\iota_\Sigma$ [Def-H.13].
**(P-H6)** FT.20's $H$-row fold: H's coarse-grained observable content is read entirely through the mass read $m_{\mathrm{hid}}$ (grounds: EW.13 $H=h\Sigma$; EW.20/EW.33 $M^2\propto h_0^2$) — a declared modelling choice; H's block $\mathcal K^{(H)}$ remains distinct inside $\mathcal K_\Sigma$ [Def-H.15, FT.28(4)].
**(P-H7)** FT.33's defect transfer: a floor fermion's pole displacement $m_{\mathrm{pole}}^2$ takes EW.33's leading-order defect form for the seed $\mathsf v$ [EW.32] weak-transported around its materialized bound chain: $m_{\mathrm{pole}}^2=\hat\mu^2\ell_c^2G(c)$, with $\hat\mu^2>0$ a floor-independent residue packaging $\varkappa^2\beta^2h_0^2$ (EW.33's $c_W$ with $\ell^2$ factored out) and uniform coupling profile along the chain (EW.33's own leading-order regime). The corpus proves the defect form for wrapping-chain gauge modes [EW.33/EW.20], not for fermion termini — the transfer is the declared content; no identification $m_{\mathrm{pole}}=m_{\mathrm{hid}}$ is asserted anywhere. (Exact form: P-H7$'$.)
**(P-H7$'$)** FT.54(4)'s exact strengthening: P-H7's defect transport is Def-H.21's spin-connection holonomy — $m_{\mathrm{pole}}^2=\hat\mu'^2\cdot4\sin^2(\Theta_R/4)$ with $\Theta_R=\iint_S K\,dA$ the Gauss–Bonnet angle defect; $[\hat\mu'^2]=\mathfrak q$ [EW.33 packaging pattern]. Leading-order match with P-H7 requires $\hat\mu'^2\Theta_R^2/4=\hat\mu^2\ell_c^2G(c)$ — recorded, not imposed. Same status as P-H7: declared transfer, open to derivation or replacement (Open Gaps 7).
**(P-H8)** Def-H.19's stability vocabulary ("jump", stationary/stable/unstable): Morse-theoretic [math] program vocabulary; asserts no coupling to materialization (Open Gaps 18). **[Sharpened (FT.61): the coupling question's selection side is unreachable from weight/ledger data — two named exits; the vocabulary itself stands.]** **[Refined: the vocabulary is upgraded to Morse–Bott on derived geometry [Def-H.24 — construction class, no new declaration]; the non-coupling clause is superseded in part by FT.66(4)'s *derived* anti-coupling for absolute index rules on closed chains; Gap 18 closed by FT.66(5), open-channel residue at item 13.]**
**(P-H9)** Def-H.23/FT.60's winding-multiplicity transfer: a materialized closed bound chain of winding $k\ge0$ carries $\mathrm{Sym}^{k}(\mathcal H_{\mathsf v})$ — $k$ symmetric copies of the EW.32 seed — as its weak-transport carrier, with the diagonal action $\mathcal U_c^{\otimes k}|_{\mathrm{Sym}^k}$. The corpus proves one seed transported per chain regardless of winding [EW.32] and the uniqueness of this realization given the rule [FT.52(3), FT.55(2), FT.56(3)]; the coupling *multiplicity $=$ winding* is the declared content — proven irreducible to subloop decomposition and load-bearing [FT.60(7b): the uniform-minimal triangle's winding $3$ has no three materialized winding-$1$ subloops]. Same species as P-H7 (a transfer around EW.32/EW.33's proven transport); open to derivation or replacement (Open Gaps 19, with the P-H7 family under 7). Scope: bound chains [D1.5], nonnegative cone [FT.52(3)]; transit/trivial chains excluded — photon helicity [EM.8] is a transit-side read, no identification; Carriers for $k\ge3$ exceed the registered pair-channel inventory [CT0.12] — flagged [FT.60(8)], no aspect claim.
**(P-H10)** FT.68(5)'s flicker equilibrium: $r:=\Gamma_{J^+}/\Gamma_{J^-}$ is a free internal *dynamics* datum (P4-class, $\neg$a constant of the theory — the corpus supplies no $J^\pm$ rates: D1.2 is transition vocabulary, A5's draws are unrecorded [inventory]), and the crystallization equilibrium is per-edge independent detailed balance with $p_{\mathrm{real}}=r/(1+r)$. **(P-H11) [CANDIDATE — proposed, NOT adopted, no result uses it.]** FT.91(4)'s minimal spectral-bridge map: for a cover class $\mathcal O$ and an observer $o\notin\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal O)$, $\Sigma(\mathcal O):=$ the vertex support of any representative $R\in\mathcal O$ (well-defined up to $\operatorname{Aut}$: $\operatorname{supp}(gR)=g\operatorname{supp}(R)$, machine-verified), $C(\mathcal O):=$ the boundary channel induced by $\gamma(o,\Sigma(\mathcal O))$ [Def-H.1], and the spectral side of $\mathcal O$ is $Z(\mathcal R,\Sigma(\mathcal O))$ with poles at $1\in\operatorname{spec}\mathbf F_M^{(\Sigma(\mathcal O))}$ [Def-H.16/CT2.5], aspects by FT.20's criteria. **Species:** the program's first *map* declaration (contrast P-H7/P-H7$'$/P-H9's transfers and P-H10's dynamics datum). **Sufficiency:** for colour-free covers this single declaration suffices — colour data is vacuous [FT.20/FT.26(2)] and materialization is needed only for the bound side [FT.80(6)]. **Costs:** the spectrum becomes observer-relative (as $m_{\mathrm{hid}}$ already is [Def-H.9/FT.3]); no fermionic poles without a materialization clause; no ceiling is lifted ($\dim\mathcal K_\Sigma\le18$ [FT.26(3)]; FT.76(3) bounds any P-H7$'$ values). **Barred:** reading $4^{|\mathcal C|}$, $\Omega$ or $Z^\pm$ as masses or eigenvalues [FT.79(5)]; reading $\delta h$ as a scale [D0.8]; taking aspects from block membership rather than FT.20/FT.27. Logged so that any future use is a declared adoption, not a silent one.
**(P-H12)** The L3–FT.16 bridge statement: the pair-level inertial read $m_i^2(q)=\Delta S(q)$ [L3(4): the entropy deficit against the A5 vacuum, dimensionless in nats] and the subnetwork-level informational mass $m_{\mathrm{hid}}=E_{\mathrm{int}}\ln2/\mathfrak h$ [FT.16/FT.44(4), dimension $\mathfrak q^{+1/2}$] are recorded as **the same object read differently** — the ledger's entropy deficit at pair level vs at subnetwork level. Status: a declared bridge, $\neg$ a derivation — no theorem of A–H relates the two reads quantitatively (the scale $\mathfrak h$ enters only the subnetwork read); no identification with the pole mass $m^2$ [D2.5] is made or implied (item 7 remains the relating-equation question, now spanning three reads). Any future quantitative use of the bridge must be logged as an adoption first.
**(P-H13)** The fermion-mass interpretation of the nonlinear tilt (γ-pass; **declared reading, NOT adopted into any A–G derivation**): the $\varepsilon_q$-dependent Gibbs tilt $\tilde{\mathcal T}_\gamma$ [D0.3] is proposed as the dynamics of a part-real, part-virtual subnetwork — a fermion in the H-layer sense [Def-H.4, P-H0]. Fully real content follows A6's linear tick (unitary; $V$-preserving [L1(2)]); part-real, part-virtual content follows the nonlinear tilt ($V$-contracting [L1(3)–(5), L2]). The fermion mass reading is the tilt's contraction rate; with one tilt application per internal edge — a **declared composition rule**, not derived — the sharp point $\gamma=1$ gives $V_{E_{\mathrm{int}}}=(\tfrac9{16})^{E_{\mathrm{int}}}V_0$ [L2], geometric in $E_{\mathrm{int}}$ [Def-H.9, FT.16]. **Species note (structural, range arithmetic only):** this reading is distinct from the two bounded skeleton reads — the pole mass $m_{\mathrm{pole}}^2\in[0,4\hat\mu'^2]$ [FT.76(3)] and the cover mass $K=2|\mathcal C|\le3V/2$ [FT.79(2)] — which bound their own species; the ceilings of item 20 deficit 12 do not constrain the tilt reading. **Barred:** any claim that $(\tfrac9{16})^{E_{\mathrm{int}}}$ reproduces an observed mass hierarchy — no calibration datum exists [P1, P5]; particle-family identification remains inadmissible [FT.77, FT.79(4)–(6), FT.84(2), FT.97(5); item 20 deficit 12 stands]; and the reading is dimensionless in nats while the bounded reads are dimensionful — comparing them requires a declared map [FT.89(2) category trap]. Logged so that any future use is a declared adoption, not a silent one.
**(P-H14)** The 1-loop bridge's declared interfaces (Def-H.31, Tier F.35; **five components, logged as one choice complex; no A–G result uses them**): **(a) Dictionary** — the Bernoulli crystallization rate is identified with the tilt strength, $p:=\gamma=1-4\varepsilon_q$ [D0.3; **(P-H15)** The topology-blind path integral's declared interfaces (Def-H.32, Tier F.36; **five components, logged as one choice complex; no A–G result uses them; no prior P-H entry is imported by any H15 theorem**): **(a) Regularization** — the budget filtration $\mathcal G_B$ ($V\le B$ nodes; $|R|\le B_K$ real edges — the ledger's own nat content [CT1.29]); every sum is finite per cell; the $B\to\infty$ limit is not constructed. **(b) Fugacity formalism** — $t,u,x_\pm,y_d,z_d,\tau_{\mathcal R/\mathcal V}$ are formal indeterminates over $\mathbb Q$; the fixed-topology slice recovers P-H14's construction identically [FT.108(6)]; computed-cell values order-one; open to derivation or replacement. **(P-H16a)** The bound-chain arena tensor product (FT.110, Tier F.37; declared interface; no A–G result uses it; no H16 theorem imports any prior P-H entry): the bound-chain arena $\mathcal H_c$ is the tensor product of two independently present structures — the seal triplet at its termini [CT0.12, CT0.7(4)] and the polygonal transfer structure along its length [FT.68(2)] — the minimal combination consistent with both being independently present; $\dim_{\mathrm{eff}}\mathcal H_c=3\times3=9$ enters exactly once, as the denominator of FT.112's mixing angle ($\delta=2/9$ with FT.109's diagonal seal coefficient $2$ the numerator). **Species:** a structural arena declaration ($\neg$a transfer, $\neg$a dynamics datum, $\neg$a map): $\mathcal H_c$ is a counting space — no inner product, no dynamics, no state, no Hamiltonian on it. **Barred:** reading $\mathcal H_c$ as a declared state space or its dimension as a state count; reading FT.113's ratios $r_i^2$ as reproducing any measured hierarchy (FT.114's exact nonzero shortfalls stand); calibration to [EXT] data [P1, P5; FT.87(11) precedent]; particle identification of the eigenvalues [items 5/12]; importing P-H9/P-H10/P-H12/P-H13/P-H14/P-H15 content into H16 theorem statements; reading the overall scale $\mu$ as declared (P4-class, undeclared). **Status:** item 7 remains open — H16 adds a ratio-level read with an undeclared overall scale, not a new absolute read; open to derivation or replacement. **(P-H18a)** The chain-carrier tensor product (FT.123, Tier F.39; declared interface; no A–G result uses it; no H18 theorem imports any P-H9–P-H17 content except as cited): the matter carrier of a bound chain extends from the seed $\mathcal H_{\mathsf v}\cong\mathbb C^2$ [EW.32] to $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{int}}\otimes\mathcal H_\omega(c)$ — the seed factor tensored with Def-H.33's orientation doublet — with the weak action on the first factor alone ($W_i\mapsto\tfrac12\sigma_i\otimes I_\omega$ [EW.32]) and the chiral grade $\hat\chi$ on the second alone. **Species:** a structural carrier declaration ($\neg$a transfer, $\neg$a dynamics datum, $\neg$a map): it names item 21(i)'s 'extended carrier' locus and makes the chain-level grade weak-commuting; the orientation space itself and every operator identity on it ($\hat\omega$, $\hat\chi$, $P_{L/R}$) are declaration-free [Def-H.33, FT.122]. **Tension logged:** FT.93(3d)'s Schur obstruction (the seed carrier is weak-irreducible: commutant $\mathbb CI_2$) is discharged by multiplicity, not violated — the extended action is seed$\otimes$trivial with commutant $I_{\mathrm{int}}\otimes\operatorname{End}(\mathcal H_\omega)$ (complex dimension $4$, machine); FT.93(3)'s pincer classifies the pair-register weak action (Casimir $2\Pi_{\mathcal R}$, type $3\oplus1$) and does not reach the extended one (Casimir $\tfrac34I_4$, type $2\oplus2$) — the actions are inequivalent (machine, FT.123(1)). Item 21(i)'s frame section $e\mapsto\mathcal F_e$, its EW.32-transport coupling, and any global Dirac-type operator remain unconstructed. **Barred:** reading $\hat\chi$'s eigenspaces as species or as SM chiralities (the L/R names are algebraic until deficit 1$'$ closes); reading the spin-structure torsor analogy as an identification (distinct $\mathbb Z_2$'s — FT.89(2) kind discipline, FT.122(7)); adopting FT.123(3)'s cardinality correspondence as a label-to-weight assignment; calibration to [EXT] data [P1, P5]; importing P-H9–P-H17 content into H18 theorem statements except as cited. **Status:** deficit 1 is closed at the projector level (FT.125(1)), with item 21(i)'s residue standing; deficit 1$'$ (chiral matter assignment) is opened. Logged so that any future use is a declared adoption, not a silent one.

The promoted read is the sole source of a chirality-flipping block: $F_{LR}=q_{\mathrm{core}}I_2$, flipping set $\{F_{\mathrm{EM}}\ \text{core read}\}$, chiral-partner splitting $2|q|\in\{0,2\}$ [FT.129(5), FT.130(5)]; at the derived locus (no promotion) the no-flip theorem holds unconditionally [FT.128]. **Species:** a structural read-to-operator promotion ($\neg$a transfer, $\neg$a dynamics datum, $\neg$a map): A–G treats reads as labels [Def-H.15(4)], so the promotion is the declared content; $q_{\mathrm{core}}$ itself stays registration data (ternary [D3.4, EM.9, FT.40]), $\neg$fitted. **Tension logged:** the chiral rays superpose $\omega$-opposed core charges $(q,-q)$ [FT.40] while channel-level coarse-graining is sign-blind and fuses only equal-$q$ states [FT.15's hypothesis; FT.90(5)'s audit] — the carrier-level doublet and channel fusion are distinct structures; their relation is item 7/item 13 bridge content, open, and no H19 theorem resolves it [FT.131(5)]. **Barred:** reading $2|q|$ as a frequency (no carrier-time parameter exists — FT.131(3): emergent time is a prerequisite, item 21(b)); reading $\hat\kappa$- or $\hat\chi$-eigenrays as species or SM chiralities (naming and assignment are deficit 1$'$ content); calibration to [EXT] data [P1, P5]; promoting $\omega$-even reads to $\hat\kappa$-multiples (the parity split [FT.129(3)] is derived, not declared); a transit-chain flipping census (out of scope [FT.131(1)]). **Status:** deficit 1$'$ gains its static datum (flipping block computed; frequency deferred pending emergent time); items 7/13 gain the logged carrier-vs-channel tension. Logged so that any future use is a declared adoption, not a silent one.

The open residue is not a derivation of the identification (none is needed) but the carrier-level parity-eigenvalue assignment to species — deficit 1$'$ content [FT.140(3)].

Any future use that needs a value must log a declared choice here first. **Logged:** $\alpha$ — the formal temperature parameter of FT.79(3)'s *Ansatz B* class mass $m_B=(K-\alpha\ln\Omega)\ln2/\mathfrak h$ [Def-H.28]. New parameters, if later formalization requires them, must be logged here before use (author's policy).

**§I.4 The revision registries and inherited premises (historical text).**

**Dissolution Registry (corpus-level premises, Tier F.33).**
**(1) EM.20 Eff — eliminated.** The per-edge phase is a measurement outcome determined by the endpoint alignment at crystallization [Def-EM.20, EM.20(2)]; no effectivity premise carries any A–G conclusion. The alphabet claim is derived: $\alpha_e\in\{0,\pi\}=\Phi$ [EM.20(1); CT0.9].
**(2) EM.20a rung stability — withdrawn.** The phase is per-edge dynamic [EM.20]; rung stability of a fixed $\alpha$ is no longer meaningful. The surviving rung-level reads: the block read is rung-stable ($\alpha_{\mathrm{EM}}^{(n)}=\alpha_{\mathrm{EM}}^{(2)}$ — EM.18/EM.19) and the alphabet is rung-blind ($\{0,\pi\}$ at every accessible rung — EM.20(1)).
**(3) CT2.8 (i)–(ii) — closed.** Reflection and factorization are derived clauses: $w(\Theta_{\mathfrak L}C)=w(C)^*$ [D2.7, D3.6, A8 — CT2.8(1)]; $w(C)=w(C_{\mathrm{left}})w(C_{\mathrm{right}})$ across interior ledger-time cuts [A8 product structure, D1.9 tick locality, D1.3 seals boundary-only — CT2.8(2)]; positivity, null space, quotient/completion, convergence: CT2.8(3)–(6) [CT2.2; D2.8; math]. No new axioms; no P-H imports.
**(4) CT2.6 (i) — reclassified as a definitional precondition.** "There is something": D2.11's valid-universe definition in application, $\neg$ an extra-axiomatic hypothesis. A §0.2a now carries exactly two items: CT2.6 (ii), CT2.6 (iii) — both the continuum limit.
**Tag ledger.** EM.23 and EM.24 carry the parity classification and the CPT bridge; the sector self-containment audit and the phase/coupling separation are consolidated into EM.25(7)–(10); the EM.20a block is removed; Def-EM.20/Def-EM.21/Def-EM.22 are registered declarations of **E**. On-shell quantization reads transfer to the antialigned content: $\Theta\to\Theta_{\mathcal P}$ [CT1.19b; FT.100 Status].

**Axiom Revision Registry (A4/A6/A7 repair pass).**
**(1) A4 — two-branch crystallization.** $\mathcal R$ admits both branches of the antisymmetric-weight test: $\bigl|\Pi_{\mathcal V}\rho_{pq}\bigr|\le\varepsilon_q$ (aligned) $\vee$ $\ge1-\varepsilon_q$ (antialigned); the branch phase $\alpha_{pq}\in\{0,\pi\}$ and both branches' non-emptiness are declared inside the axiom. Branch disjointness ($\varepsilon_q<1-\varepsilon_q$) is a scope condition carried by downstream theorems [CT0.8: the admissible range $[0,\tfrac14)$ guarantees it and keeps the A5 vacuum virtual in both branches]. The per-edge phase determination is the branch index: $\alpha_e=\alpha_{pq}$ [A7 map; Def-EM.20; EM.20(2)] — the phase reads of Tier F.33 (Def-EM.20, EM.20, EM.23, EM.24) carry through unchanged at the tag level; their mechanism clause now cites the A4 branch assignment.
**(2) A6 — Haar tick dynamics.** The axiom declares $\mu_\star$, the tick map $\mathcal T(\rho)=U\rho U^\dagger$ (invertible: $\mathcal T^{-1}(\rho)=U^\dagger\rho U$), trace preservation, $\mathcal T^2\ne\mathbb1$, the ledger-count recursion $|\mathfrak L(\tau_{n+1})|=|\mathfrak L(\tau_n)|+n_{\mathrm{real\_out}}(\tau_n)$, and tick irreversibility ($\neg\exists\,\tau^{-1}$) — the map is invertible, the sequence is not. The thermal map and its variance clause are no longer axiomatic: the Gibbs tilt survives as D0.3 vocabulary ($\tilde{\mathcal T}$, $\ne\mathcal T$, not interchangeable) and is consumed by L1; no theorem of the corpus depended on the removed clauses (D0.3's own reservation note).
**(3) A7 — graph + ledger + phase map.** A7 keeps the graph/type/register structure and adds the ledger stratification $\mathfrak L=\bigcup_n\mathfrak L(\tau_n)$ with $|\mathfrak L(\tau_0)|=0$ and the closed-form count $\sum_{k<n}n_{\mathrm{real\_out}}(\tau_k)$, plus the edge phase map $e\in E_{\mathcal R}\mapsto\alpha_e\in\{0,\pi\}$ — the determination (A4's branch assignment) is external to the axiom block.
**(4) Lyapunov restoration — L1, L2, L3 (B, Tier 2).** Theorems, $\neg$ axioms: $V(\tilde K)=\mathfrak T(\tilde K^2)$ with $\tilde K=K-\mathfrak T(K\rho)I$ is a Lyapunov function of the pair state — tick-invariant under A6's Haar conjugation, strictly decreased by the D0.3 Gibbs tilt with the exact per-eigenvalue deficit $\phi(x)=2x\log(1+4x)-x^2$ (identity exact through third order, quartic remainder machine-pinned; $\phi''=(14+16x-32x^2)/(1+4x)^2$ exact; convexity window $[\tfrac{1-2\sqrt2}{4},\tfrac{1+2\sqrt2}{4}]\supseteq[-\tfrac14,\tfrac34]$; Jensen; global monotonicity by exact rational log-enclosures), decay law $V'-V=-\tfrac7{16}V+O(\delta\rho^3)$, entropy deficit $\Delta S_{\mathrm{pair}}=2|\delta\rho|^2=\tfrac12|r|^2$, and the inertial read $m_i^2(q)=\Delta S(q)$ — bridge to $m_{\mathrm{hid}}$: P-H12 (declared, not derived). **Vocabulary supersession:** FT.85(3)'s non-constructibility adjudication — 'inertial', 'Lyapunov', 'entropy deficit' count 0 in A–G, $m_i^2:=\Delta S$ not constructible — is superseded by this restoration for the single read $m_i^2(q)=\Delta S(q)$ [L3(4)]; the checker pins are updated accordingly (`verify_H_gravmass.py` §7, `verify_H_tumbling.py` §G). The remainder of FT.85(3)/FT.86(8) stands: no gravitational counterpart $m_g^2$, no equivalence theorem, no clock-rate/field coupling, no maximal mixing; the tumbling-family vocabulary remains absent from A–G. Machine: `checks/verify_L_lyapunov.py`.

**Tilt Revision Registry ($\varepsilon_q$-dependent Gibbs tilt; D0.3/L1–L3 γ-pass).**
**(1) D0.3.** The Gibbs tilt carries the strength $\gamma:=1-4\varepsilon_q\in(0,1]$ [A4, D0.4, CT0.8]: $\tilde{\mathcal T}_\gamma(\rho)=\rho e^{-\gamma\rho}/\mathfrak T(\rho e^{-\gamma\rho})$; $\tilde{\mathcal T}_\gamma(K)=K\rho^\gamma/\mathfrak T(K\rho^\gamma)$; at $\varepsilon_q=0$ (sharp crystallization) $\gamma=1$; the identity-on-states limit sits at the excluded endpoint $\varepsilon_q=\tfrac14$ ($\gamma\to0$). The variance functional, the Lyapunov functional $V(\tilde K)$ and the centered surprisal $\tilde K$ are unchanged; $\tilde{\mathcal T}_\gamma\ne\mathcal T$ [A6].
**(2) L1–L3.** Decay identity $V'-V=-\gamma\sum_i\phi_\gamma(\mu_i)+O(\delta\rho^4)$ with $\phi_\gamma(x)=2x\log(1+4x)-\gamma x^2$ (exact through third order at every rational $\gamma$; sharp quartic remainder machine-pinned); $\phi_\gamma''=(14+16x-32x^2)/(1+4x)^2+2(1-\gamma)$; $\phi_\gamma=\phi_1+(1-\gamma)x^2$ (positivity and convexity inherited from the $\gamma=1$ case for every $\gamma\in(0,1]$); decay law $V'-V=-[1-(1-\tfrac\gamma4)^2]V=-\tfrac{\gamma(8-\gamma)}{16}V+O(\delta\rho^3)$; eigenvalue contraction $\mu'=(1-\tfrac\gamma4)\mu+O(\mu^2)$; deficit decay $V'-V=-\tfrac{\gamma(8-\gamma)}2\Delta S_{\mathrm{pair}}+O(\delta\rho^3)$. The sharp-crystallization values $\tfrac9{16}$/$\tfrac7{16}$/$\tfrac34$/$\tfrac72$ of the Axiom Revision Registry item (4) are the $\gamma=1$ points — the $7/16$ is derived from $\varepsilon_q=0$ [D0.4], $\neg$ stipulated. A7 left L3's Req: the inertial read is definitional at pair level.
**(3) Fermion-mass interpretation.** Logged as the declared reading **P-H13** — including the per-internal-edge composition rule $V_{E_{\mathrm{int}}}=(\tfrac9{16})^{E_{\mathrm{int}}}V_0$ at $\gamma=1$, the species distinction from the bounded pole/cover reads, and the barred hierarchy/identification claims. No A–G theorem carries it.
**(4) Extra-axiomatic list.** Unchanged: CT2.6 (ii), CT2.6 (iii) — two items, both the continuum limit [A §0.2a].

**Legacy dynamics import registry (ledger–tilt and branch-grading pass).**
**(1) L0 — the ledger–tilt coupling (B, Tier 2).** The D0.3 tilt is coupled to the A4 events: one tilt step per crystallization event; the tick is $V$-preserving [L1(2)], the tilt is $V$-contracting [L1(5)], the ledger count $|\mathfrak L(\tau_n)|=\sum_{k<n}n_{\mathrm{real\_out}}(\tau_k)$ [A6, A7] is the tilt-step count, and interleaved Haar draws are spectrally inert — the L1–L3 contraction is the per-increment dynamics of the ledger, with deficit fraction $\gamma(8-\gamma)/16$ per step [L2]. The coupling itself is a declared theorem-local dynamical scope (CT4.5a precedent; recorded in A §0.2a's integration note) — A4–A7 declare the criterion, the tick and the ledger, not a seal-time state update. **(A-BM.12) The momentum-space resolvent interface (Tier BM.23).** *Species:* the interface declaration BM.6(6) and item 17 named as the resolvent's blocker — the minimal non-derived content of Def-BM.20: (i) the boundary-channel embedding (the identification of the boundary-channel register's momentum label with the chart's $p_\mu$ on CT2.6's arena, an $\iota_\Sigma$-type embedding at the momentum level); (ii) the Fourier normalization ($d^3p/(2\pi)^3$ per chart dimension); (iii) the minimal vertex normalization $\mathcal V=1$, constituted by its reduction clause (at $e=1$, $q=1$ every amplitude reduces to the register's channel weight [BM.122(3)]). *Sufficiency:* everything else in Def-BM.20/BM.134 is forced — the denominator is the derived mass-shell operator [BM.34(4)], and BM.134(2)'s form-uniqueness theorem pins the resolvent among all rational forms of its degree on five derived conditions (pole locus, vacuum-gauge reduction, T-evenness, hermitian sign-freeness, simple poles); the phase space is the delta reduction of the chart measure [BM.134(3),(4)]. *Costs:* none — no scale, no P4 member; any other Fourier or vertex normalization rescales the amplitude reads by a declared constant, an $e_0$-species calibration move, $\neg$ structure; CT2.6's hypotheses are inherited at every point of use [A §0.2a]. *Bars:* P5 stands absolutely — $\neg$a numerical cross-section, $\neg$a measured comparison, $\neg$an S-matrix unitarity claim, $\neg$an $i\varepsilon$-prescription import (the read is meromorphic, sign-free off the poles [FT.26(1) pattern]); amplitude $\ne$ mass [FT.79(5)]; the chart resolvent and the register's zig-zag resolvent stay separated species meeting only at the threshold indicator [BM.134(5)]; the chart-frequency width is $\neg$formed at $\mathcal V=1$ on the dimensional census, and the per-tick ledger species stays the formed width read [BM.142(7); BM.44(5)]. *Status:* **DECLARED AND LOGGED** before use; item 17 is closed by BM.134. Machine: `checks/verify_H_bm_closing_tier.py` §C, §H.

**(A-BM.13) The statistics locus, $\vee^2$ (Tier BM.23).** *Species:* the one non-derived row BM.118(4) priced and left undeclared — Def-BM.16(5)'s choice of the $\vee^2$-sector as the locus of the permutation-sign read $\varepsilon(c)$. **Issued: the $\vee^2$-sector is the standing locus.** *Sufficiency:* the four alternatives are computed and priced [BM.113(6)]: $\wedge^2$ gives the same statistics up to a global sign (a loci-doubling with no new content), while the determinant line and the orientation block fail the statistics outright ($(-1)^{|k|(|k|+1)/2}\ne(-1)^{|k|}$ at $k=2$; the orientation block is statistics-blind with $\operatorname{Tr}=0$); $\vee^2$ is the flip's $+1$-eigensector on the CP pair [A3] and matches the carrier's own symmetry — $\wedge^3(\mathbb C^2)=0$ leaves the carrier no antisymmetric locus at $n=3$ [BM.128(5)]. *Costs:* none — no scale, no P4 member, no measured value; every derived statistics row ($\varepsilon=(-1)^{|k|}=(-1)^{2j}$, $\varepsilon_M=+1$, $\varepsilon_B=(-1)^{k_1+k_2+k_3}$, $\varepsilon(\Gamma)=\prod_a\varepsilon(c_a)$) is invariant under the issuance. *Bars:* P5 stands; the FT.125(5)/FT.89(2) kind bar is respected — $\varepsilon$ remains a permutation sign, $\neg$one of the four single-object $\mathbb Z_2$ gradings; deficit 11's closure [BM.113(7)] is untouched. *Status:* **DECLARED AND LOGGED** before use; the "priced, $\neg$forced, $\neg$declared" row of BM.118(7) is discharged. Machine: `checks/verify_H_bm_closing_tier.py` §H.

### FT.148 — The structural/census separation of the working layer

**Thm.** Every theorem of H01–H21 is separated into exactly one of two categories:
$$(1)\quad\textbf{the classification rule:}\qquad\text{Category A (structural)}\iff\text{the tag's primary deliverable is an identity, mechanism, covariance law, no-go, uniqueness, or scope adjudication about the corpus's algebra, geometry or dynamics}\ (\text{even when proved by exhaustive enumeration});$$
$$\text{Category B (data-table census)}\iff\text{the tag's primary deliverable is a computed table, card, ladder, orbit set, multiplet family or count over a named configuration space, and downstream consumers cite the tabulated values};$$
$$\text{mixed tags are classified by primary deliverable; a census side of an A tag is cross-referenced in the H-CENSUS preamble}\ [\text{FT.68},\ \text{FT.93},\ \text{FT.103}\ \text{rows}];$$
$$(2)\quad\textbf{the separation table}\ (\text{all}\ 145\ \text{theorems; machine: each tag exactly once, categories disjoint and complete}):$$

**Phys.** The baryonic era does not start from zero and it does not start from hope. The colour algebra, the sealing grammar, and the lock side are frozen; the first colour-active cells are already computed, with their junction phases and their four-fold winding ledgers; the channel machinery that produced three family angles runs on any arena the era can derive; and twenty-five census supplies stand labeled with the spaces they enumerate. What the era must add is short and named: a domain choice here, a convention there, rates if it wants dynamics, an observer if it wants spectra — each a logged declaration at its point of use, none issued in advance. The rung closes with its debts itemized.

**Status.** Closed; preparation content only — no construction, no declaration, no identification. Machine: §E.

The content checkers are unchanged in their mathematics; their document pins resolve against the bundled first editions (`H_archive/`) and the era files. Suite state at delivery: recorded in `checks/README.md`.

**(V.4) The Tier F.44 pass.** The era canon gains Tier F.44 (FT.156–FT.160 — the 3+1 chiral asymmetry: the signature datum at two loci with the reversal transport and the inner triad symmetry; the weak block as the metric's shift block with the spatial/temporal branch selectivity; the spatial provenance of the channel angle with the supersession of FT.89's classification clause; the emergent-time identity removing item 21(b)'s ``no emergent time'' blockage), the on-record Rmk at FT.89, and the §II ledger brackets. Its content checker is `checks/verify_H_3p1_chiral_asymmetry.py` (§A–§E; exact arithmetic only: $\mathbb Q(i)$ register and rotation identities, exact rational $\mathrm{LDL}^\top$ inertia witnesses, $\mathbb Q(\sqrt2,\sqrt3)[c,s]/(c^2{+}s^2{-}1)$ spectral identities, $\mathbb Z[\sqrt6]$, exhaustive ledger-word and weight-conjugation enumerations, document pins). Tier F.44 is new canon content with no first edition in `H_archive/`, so its checker pins the era-canon texts directly — the transplant-pin pattern of (V.1) does not apply to it. The tier series continues at F.44 (F.43 is the historical closure tier, §IV.3). Suite state: 58/58. The index (`index/`) and the Summary of Record (`Summary.md`) are regenerated.

**(V.5) The render-tooling repair.** `render_paper.py` receives three tooling repairs (no canon text touched; the rendered artifacts are regenerated with the repaired script): (a) the module docstring is raw — Python 3.12+ raised `SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\['` on the `\[...\]` pipeline description; (b) a failed compile on a non-tectonic engine prints the first fatal error blocks from the engine's log (plus the stderr tail) in place of the bare ``compile failed'' line, and the multi-pass loop aborts at the first fatal pass — the swallowed-error behavior left local-engine shortfalls (a minimal TeX Live missing packages such as `titlesec.sty`/`microtype.sty`, or missing Latin Modern OpenType fonts for `fontspec`) undiagnosable at the terminal; (c) the failure message carries the two remediation hints (the self-contained engine via `--fetch-engine`; installing the packages named in the errors). The success path, the repair loop, and the rendered output are unchanged.

**(V.6) The intelligent line breaking of rendered displays.** `render_paper.py`'s font-shrink ladder for wide displays is replaced by a break-first ladder (renderer-side only — no canon text is touched; both paper variants and the Summary PDF are regenerated with it). A too-wide display is broken into stacked displays at the corpus's own top-level clause separators — stage 1 `\qquad`, stage 2 `\quad`, stage 3 top-level `;`, `,` and relations (`=`, `\implies`, `\iff`, `\mapsto`, `\equiv`, `\ne`/`\neq`, `\le`, `\ge`, `\subseteq`, `\in`, the arrow macros) — under depth tracking that never breaks inside braces, `\begin..\end` environments, `\left..\right`/`\bigl..\bigr` groups, or parentheses/brackets (interval notation such as `(0,1]` counts as balanced, so it does not suppress breaks). Breaking runs proactively at build time (measured with the table-width estimator, threshold 80 visible characters) and reactively on the residual overfulls; only fragments still too wide after every stage enter the former ladder (`\small` → `\footnotesize` → `\scriptsize` → `\resizebox`), and split pieces now walk that ladder gently instead of jumping to `\resizebox`. Measured effect on the canon-only paper (same canon, old ladder vs new): first-round overfull displays $1716\to546$; `\resizebox`-squashed displays $700\to209$ (the residue: wide in-display arrays and unbreakable monoliths); `\footnotesize` wraps $385\to126$; level-3 marks $2245\to71$; full-size displays $\approx96\%$ ($6739$ of $7077$); residual overfull lines in the final log $114\to63$; the repair loop converges at round 6.

**(V.13) The Tier BM.9 pass — the mesonic flicker test.** The era canon gains Tier BM.9 in Part 4 (Def-BM.9, BM.31–BM.33). The tier answers a posed question rather than asserting an answer, and the answer is a negative with an unusual provenance: $Z^{(n)}_\delta=0$ **by filtration, not by cancellation**. The grammar is enumerated rather than cited — exhaustive set partitions into blocks of size 2 and 3 reproduce CT3.17a(2)/FT.41(b)'s frozen $g(N)=(1,1,3,10,25,105)$ for $N\le7$ and split it into $g_\delta=(1,0,3,10,15,105)$ and $g_\varepsilon$ (nonzero exactly where $3\mid N$) — so $g(3)=1$ with $g_\delta(3)=0$: a 3-attachment support admits exactly one contraction, a $\delta$ sub-pairing leaves one dangling attachment, and D2.14's projector with FT.19's all-or-nothing law annihilates the block. and **$\mathcal T\in(0,\infty)$ at every admissible lapse, so every locus is a nonempty half-line and ¬a permanently forbidden state exists** — all ordered constituent triples are admissible threshold-wise, the two heaviest-floor triples being unread and ¬unreachable. **The width $\Gamma=\Gamma_{\mathrm{tr}}f(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eff}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{EM}})\mathcal N\chi$ has three exact zeros with three distinct mechanisms** — no flicker ($\varepsilon_q=0$), the member's own envelope ($f(1)=0$), and the class's lightest member ($\mathcal N=0$, three at $n=2$ and seven at $n=3$) — **plus the indicator's zero, which is absence and ¬stability**, with $0\le\Gamma\le\tfrac13N\mathsf g\mathcal N$ and **$\tau_{\mathrm{life}}\ge3$ ticks**; $E\uparrow\Rightarrow k\uparrow\Rightarrow f\uparrow\Rightarrow\Gamma\uparrow$ monotonically, so "low" and "high" energy are two calibration points on one function. **The groupings are eight derived families and the octet is ¬formed** — $8$ occurs in none at any formed level — while $15=\binom62$ and $20=\binom63$ are orbit counts and ¬representation dimensions; **the ceiling is $\lambda^{(n)}_{\max}=4n$ attained exactly once**, a sum of single-chain reads so ¬a ceiling is violated and ¬extended, and deficit 12 stands with no fourth ceiling added. **Both standing orders are carried as scope clauses:** ¬a cosmological coordinate exists (the ledger owns the tick, the curvature, the lapse and the scale, and no global time), and ¬a binary stable/unstable category exists (**every eigenvalue is a resonance**, the adjectives being values of the width function). **Two residues are added — (R8) the $2$-chain sector masses and (R9) the annihilation channel — and (R7) narrows to $n\ge3$**; nothing measured entered anywhere, $\alpha_B$ stays barred, and no currency is converted. Its content checker is `checks/verify_H_antimatter_resonance_spectrum.py` (§A–§G; exact arithmetic only: the degree-16 algebra $\mathbb M$ with basis-coordinate equality, a rigorous dyadic interval embedding built from integer square roots for exact ordering, exact Fraction Gaussian elimination for the minimal polynomials, exact complex over Fraction for the exchange matrices, integer and Laurent-polynomial identities, the SOS parabola completion, exhaustive orbit, charge and grid censuses, document pins). Tier BM.21 is new canon content with no first edition, so its checker pins the era-canon texts directly. **The same pass records the externalization of `H_archive/`:** the 21 bundled first editions moved to a separate historical-archive repository tracing the theory from its roots, so they are no longer the identity base available here. Their pins are real verification and were therefore **gated, not deleted** — `era_paths.py` gained `ARCHIVE_PRESENT`, `ABSENT` and `archive_note()`, `read()` yields `""` for an absent first edition and records it rather than raising, and 15 content checkers wrap their first-edition sections in `if ARCH:` with one load-time `NOTE:` line each, so the pins are skipped here and run again unchanged wherever the archive is bundled. The gating is flow-sensitive (a name is archive-tainted from a tainted binding until rebound; gating a whole loop taints what it accumulates), so no era-canon assertion is skipped by association: **196 of the 1269 assertions in those 15 files are archive-scoped; the other 1073 — every assertion about this repository's era canon — runs unconditionally.** Two mixed statements were split rather than gated so their era-canon halves stay live, and the gating is reproducible: `checks/gate_archive_pins.py` re-derives it and refuses to write unless four audits pass (parses, runs disjoint, no gated assertion references an era-canon document, and no name bound inside a gate is referenced outside one). Reversibility was verified: with an `H_archive/` present the gated pins execute again unchanged. `verify_H_format.py` is **retired** — every one of its checks read the concatenation of H01–H21, so the externalization left it no subject matter, and its coverage (block completeness, citation closure, `$$` and brace balance, tag ownership, declaration hygiene) is enforced against the era canon by `check_format.py` and `verify_era_structure.py`, which is now the sole authority here. The tier's own checker replaces it in the discovered set, so **the suite count is unchanged at 76 and the suite state is 76/76**. **(V.26) The Tier BM.22 pass — the annihilation channel, the concrete spin and the fermion identification.** The era canon gains Tier BM.22 in Part 4 (Def-BM.18–Def-BM.19 and BM.119–BM.130). **The tier issues no declaration, adds no P4 member and no dimensionful scale**; §Cal-BM keeps A-BM.9's slot with its value ¬issued. Its constitutive clauses are constructions on derived objects — the Janus register and its two D1.2 moves, and the locus at which the spin enters the transport defect, which FT.55(1)'s no-go forces rather than leaves open. **(i) The c-threshold adjudication (BM.119):** $c=1$ is a unit convention carrying ¬an inequality, and BM.34(4)'s dispersion gives the exact strict deficit $1-v=m^2/(E(E+p))>0$ from the cleared identity $(E-p)(E+p)=m^2$, so with $m_{\mathrm{hid}}\ge2\ln2/\mathfrak h>0$ on the two-bit floor **no materialized chain reaches $c$ at any finite momentum** and three velocity readings are ¬formed. **The derived boundary is the Janus register:** $n_{\mathcal R}=0\Rightarrow\rho_\circ=\tfrac14I$ at the unique raw-entropy maximum $\ln4$ and $|E_{\mathcal R}|=0\Rightarrow$ ¬a fermion terminus, so crossing is the move $\hat J_\pm$ with a threshold in $\varepsilon_q$ and ¬an acceleration. **(ii) The Janus tunnel (Def-BM.18, BM.120):** on $\mathcal J\cong\mathbb C^2$ the two moves satisfy **$\hat J_\pm^2=0$, $\hat J_+\hat J_-=P_{\mathcal V}$, $\hat J_-\hat J_+=P_{\mathcal R}$, $\{\hat J_+,\hat J_-\}=\mathrm{id}$**, and FT.108(4)'s generator decomposes as **$Q=x_+(\hat J_+-P_{\mathcal V})+x_-(\hat J_--P_{\mathcal R})$** with zero row sums — **a master generator and ¬a unitary**. $\det Q=0\ne Q$ gives $\operatorname{rk}=1$, $\dim\ker Q=1$, $p=x_+/(x_++x_-)=1-4\varepsilon_q$ identically and **detailed balance as an identity**; $\operatorname{spec}Q=\{0,-(x_++x_-)\}=\{0,-1\}$ with eigenvectors $(x_-,x_+)$ and $(1,-1)$. **The round-trip maximum coincides exactly with the detailed-balance point**, $x_+x_-\le\tfrac14$ by AM–GM with equality $\iff x_+=x_-\iff\varepsilon_q=\tfrac18$. **The type correction is derived:** $\hat\omega^2=\mathrm{id}$ against $\hat J_\pm^2=0$, so ¬an algebra isomorphism $\mathcal J\cong\mathcal H_\omega$ exists and identifying the real/virtual register with the orientation doublet is a barred kind confusion. $\mathcal T_{\mathcal J}(c)=\mathcal J\otimes\mathcal H_\omega\otimes\Psi$ has $\dim=4(|k|+1)$. **(iii) The zig-zag (BM.121):** $(\hat U^{2m})_{\varnothing\varnothing}=(x_+x_-)^m$ and $(\hat U^{2m+1})_{\varnothing\varnothing}=0$ because a return needs equal move counts while $\hat J_\pm^2=0$ forces strict alternation, so **$1/(1-w^2x_+x_-)$ is the zig-zag sum with every odd coefficient exactly zero**, term $m$ weighted $(x_+x_-)^m\le4^{-m}$ with equality at $\varepsilon_q=\tfrac18$ for every $m$, both endpoints annihilating the ladder and the pole at $|w|\ge2$ never entering the unit disc. **No backward leg on three grounds** — ¬$\exists\tau^{-1}$; the pure reversal inadmissible at its first event; $\Theta_{\mathfrak L}((J^+J^-)^m)=(J^+J^-)^m$ so the reversed reading is the same word — and the second leg carries the CP-conjugate configuration forward with $|w(c^{\mathrm{CP}})|^2=|w(c)|^2$ and $\mu_{\mathrm{CP}}=1$ on $\Theta\in2\mathbb Z$. A round trip is closed on the register and non-decreasing on the record. **BM.109(2)'s $\mathcal W_{\text{pair}}$ is re-scoped as the round-trip weight**, the one-way weights being $x_+$ and $x_-$. **(iv) The pair channel and the two-photon bar (BM.122):** charge balances exactly ($Q=q+(-q)=0$, the compensator unique, three of nine ordered charge pairs formed), the threshold is $\mathcal T_0=2E_{\mathrm{int}}\mathfrak h/\ln2$ — **$4\mathfrak h/\ln2$ at the digon floor, BM.115(2)(i)'s $n{=}2$ minimum** — and the weight $x_+^2\chi_{\{NE\ge\mathcal T_0\}}$ has exact opposite limits at the two ends of the admissible range. **Three derived CP legs:** the nilpotent weak step $\widehat V=E_+\otimes P_L$ with $\widehat V^2=0$, $\operatorname{rk}1$, $\dim\ker3$, $[W_3\otimes I_\omega,\widehat V]=\widehat V$ and image $E_+\otimes P_R$ on the opposite ray; $\mu_{\mathrm{CP}}$ with $\hat\omega P_L\hat\omega^{-1}=P_R$; and the local scale against $\mathcal T_0$. **The two-photon supply vertex is ¬constructed on four grounds** — ¬a gauged chiral coupling, deficit $1'$, ¬a momentum-space $\Sigma$-resolvent, and the D3.7 trivial chain's exclusion from Def-H.38's scope — so **the channel is derived and the amplitude ¬formed**, recorded in a two-column census. **(v) Annihilation and the closure of (R9) (BM.123):** $\hat J_-$ on both registers with $\mathcal W_{\text{ann}}=x_-^2\chi=16\varepsilon_q^2\chi$, and **direction ratio $(x_-/x_+)^2$ equal to one precisely at $\varepsilon_q=\tfrac18$**, zero at $\varepsilon_q=0$ and divergent near $\tfrac14^-$. The content returns to the $\mathcal V$ sector at $\rho_\circ=\tfrac14I$ with $S_{\mathfrak L}\to0$ and **¬a radiation leg carrying it**. **BM.27(7)/BM.29(5)/Def-BM.15(5)/BM.108(7)/BM.109(5) are frozen on their own loci and re-scoped, ¬retracted** — ¬a *direct* inter-sector operator exists, and what is constructed is a *mediated* channel $\varnothing\xrightarrow{\hat J_+\otimes\hat J_+}\{c,c^{\mathrm{CP}}\}\xrightarrow{\hat J_-\otimes\hat J_-}\varnothing$ of weight $x_+^2x_-^2$ with ¬a cross-section attached. **(R9) closes at the channel level and narrows to (R9$'$) the cross-section and (R9$''$) the radiation legs.** **(vi) The concrete spin (Def-BM.19, BM.124–BM.125):** **$\Theta=2k=4j$ exactly on the nonnegative cone**, $j=|\Theta|/4$, multiplet $\#m=k+1=2j+1$, Casimir $j(j+1)$ and stretched total $2j$. **The defect is a ladder and ¬a number:** $\Delta(m)=\|(\mathcal U^{(k)}-1)\psi_m\|^2=2-2\cos(m\Theta_R/2)=4\sin^2(m\Theta_R/4)=2-2T_{|m|}(\cos(\Theta_R/2))$ with the Chebyshev recursion verified to $m=8$ and the witness $T_m(\tfrac12)=\cos(m\pi/3)$; **a single-valued $\Delta(j)$ is ¬formed since $2j+1\ge2$**; at $j=\tfrac12$ nothing splits and at $j=1$ the ladder splits into three with an exact zero in the middle. **$\Theta_R=2\pi k$ is impossible on a dimension count** ($[k]=\mathfrak q^{+1}$, $[\Theta_R]=\mathfrak q^0$), so the two angles are ¬proportional and $\Delta$ is a function of $(m,\Theta_R)$; **the ladder carries a massless weight precisely when $\varepsilon=+1$**. $m^2_{\mathrm{pole}}(m)=\hat\mu'^2\Delta(m)$ with $0\le m^2_{\mathrm{pole}}\le4\hat\mu'^2$ attained at $m\Theta_R\equiv2\pi\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 4\pi)$, the two Def-H.37 clauses joined only by $\hat\mu'^2\Theta_R^2/4=\hat\mu^2\ell_c^2G(c)$ forcing $[\ell_c^2G]=\mathfrak q^{-1}$, and **the product form $\hat\mu'\ell_c\sqrt{G\Delta}$ ¬formed on three grounds**. The standing zero is a massless weight and ¬a massless species: BM.96(3),(4)'s strict $\nu$-positivity stands re-cited and ¬extended. **(vii) The spin-gated tunnel refuted (BM.126):** the crossing weight's alphabet is $\{\varepsilon_q\}$ alone, and **hypothesis (W7) falls to the four standing grounds** that already refuted (W) and (W6) — disjoint alphabets, ¬a count$\to$strength coupling, ¬a loop, one scale. **What replaces the prohibition is a domain row:** $\varepsilon$ requires $\Psi=\mathrm{Sym}^{|k|}$ hence a materialized closed bound chain, so transit and trivial chains carry ¬an $\varepsilon$ while the register's moves still apply; the materialization restriction is a count ($|E_{\mathcal R}|\ge2$) and the derived dichotomy is bound against transit, with BM.106(1)'s spin-parity refutation standing beside it. **$\varepsilon$ is defined on the crossing's output and ¬on its rate.** **(viii) The identification (BM.127):** $\varepsilon=(-1)^{|k|}=(-1)^{2j}$ with $k=\kappa$ registration data, so **the parity is a ledger read** — ¬a postulate, ¬a declared assignment, ¬a measurement — and it is separated from D1.5's kind label by the floor census ($\kappa=1\Rightarrow\varepsilon=-1$, $\kappa=2\Rightarrow\varepsilon=+1$ on one kind), which is exactly what makes the separation a theorem rather than a contradiction. "Fermion"/"boson" are used as reads of $\varepsilon$ and as D1.5 vocabulary with ¬a species name on either. **Complete as a function, open as a value per named class** ((R3)/deficit $1'$, the connector winding, the $k\ge3$ aspect bar). **(ix) The three-chain exchange structure (BM.128–BM.129):** $\mathcal H_3=\bigotimes_a(\mathcal H_\omega\otimes\Psi)_{c_a}$ with $\dim=8\prod(|k_a|+1)$ (64, 216, 96, 192 at the anchors); $\hat P_\sigma\hat P_\tau=\hat P_{\sigma\tau}$ on all 36 pairs; **the character formula $\chi(\sigma)=\operatorname{Tr}\hat P_\sigma=d^{\,c(\sigma)}$** on all permutations at $(n,d)\in\{2,3\}^2$, giving $8,4,2$; and **$\dim\wedge^3(\mathbb C^2)=\binom23=0$ against $\dim\wedge^3(\mathbb C^3)=\binom33=1$**, both confirmed by explicit antisymmetrizers of rank 0 and rank 1 — **three copies of the seed carrier admit no totally antisymmetric state while three colour slots admit exactly one, so the baryon's antisymmetry is colour's (¬observable by CT3.22) and the statistics is the winding's**, sharpening BM.114(2). With $d=0$ at an open connector (an open chain carries $S=0$), **$\varepsilon(\Gamma)=\prod_a\varepsilon(c_a)$** on all 125 triples and 25 pairs, giving **the meson's unconditional boson sign $\varepsilon_M=(-1)^{2k}=+1$ with $j_M=k\in\mathbb Z$** — robust also under the alternative $k\mapsto-k$ orientation reading, which agrees on the sign and differs on the spin — $\varepsilon_M=(-1)^d$ with a closed connector, and **the baryon's exact criterion $\varepsilon_B=-1\iff\#\{a:k_a\text{ odd}\}$ odd** (62 of 125; the all-$k{=}1$ anchor gives $j_B=\tfrac32$, $\varepsilon_B=-1$; the digon floor has $k=1$ odd). **(R7) is discharged at $n=3$ on all four members and narrows to (R7$'$) for $n\ge4$; (R10) the connector winding is named.** ¬a flavour $\mathsf{SU}(3)$/$\mathsf{SU}(6)$: $S_3$ permutes constituent slots and BM.117(2),(3)'s orbit counts are ¬promoted to multiplets. **No scale is fixed, no measured coupling, mass, width, lifetime, cross-section or branching fraction enters, no currency is converted, $\alpha_B$ stays barred, and no right-ray matter line, seesaw-type mechanism, Yukawa-type object or external mixing matrix is added. No cosmological coordinate is used and no black-hole physics is derived:** the accelerator and deep-gravity readings are evaluations of one threshold function at local $(E,N,G)$ with $r_s$ quarantined as P3 requires, and "low"/"high" energy remain calibration points. Its content checker is `checks/verify_H_janus_tunnel_concrete_spin.py` (§A–§F; exact arithmetic only: $2\times2$ matrix algebra over $\mathbb Z[x_+,x_-]$ and $\mathbb Q(i)$, truncated power series in $\mathbb Q[w]/(w^{12})$, the SOS parabola completion and AM–GM with equality loci on exact rationals, exact rank by Fraction elimination on the $8\times8$ and $27\times27$ antisymmetrizers, the Chebyshev recursion over $\mathbb Z[x]$ with a rational witness, $\mathfrak q$-exponent censuses, permutation-group enumeration, exhaustive parity, triple and grid censuses, document pins). **(V.27) The render-tooling repair: three display-delimiter defects and the build-hygiene guard.** Nine tooling-side and source-side items; the mathematics of no tag is altered, and every repaired site is a *delimiter*, not content. **(a) The failure.** `render_paper.py` halted the engine with `! Display math should end with $$` at a line inside BM.34's proof, and a second, self-perpetuating failure followed: an interrupted build leaves a **truncated `.aux`**, and the next build dies reading it at `\begin{document}` with `Runaway argument? {{4.21.4}{1155}{\texorpdfstring {BM.97 …` / `! File ended while scanning use of \@newl@bel` — a message that points at the preamble and not at the cause. **(b) Three source defects, all invisible to the old validator.** `check_format.py` counted `$$` **per line**, so a line holding a single `$` contributed nothing and the file passed: **(i)** 96 display delimiters written as two lines each holding one `$` instead of one line holding `$$` — 82 in Part 1, 8 in Part 2, 6 in Part 4; **(ii)** 30 bare inline `$…$` delimiters sitting directly in math mode inside 14 Part-4 displays (the `\textbf{…}\ $\sigma$\textbf{-vertex}` shape), which end a `\[ … \]` display early — Parts 1–3 and Census carried none, so Part 4 had drifted from the corpus's own convention; **(iii)** 17 clause-range dashes written with the delimiter itself, `(1)$$–$$(5)` and `(1)$–$(3)`, which close and reopen a display mid-clause. **(c) The repairs.** Every lone-`$` line became `$$`; the bare inline delimiters were dropped (the content is already in math mode, so the rendering is unchanged); the dash forms became the corpus's own `\text{--}`. Verified against `xelatex` before and after: a bare `$…$` in a display is fatal, the same `$…$` inside `\text{}`/`\textbf{}`/`\emph{}` is legal and was preserved, and the delimiters removed render identically. After the repair `render_paper.py` reports **zero** hygiene fixes outstanding, so the canon and the renderer now agree. **(d) `check_format.py` hardened** so none of the three classes can pass silently again: it flags any line whose only content is `$`, any line with an odd number of unescaped `$`, and any display containing a bare `$…$` outside a text-mode argument — the last with the fix stated in the message. **(e) `render_paper.py` hardened, in depth.** A `desugar_inline_math` pass drops math-mode `$` delimiters inside displays while preserving those in `\text{}`-family arguments, and the display collector normalises a split `$$` before parsing, reporting both counts. A **pre-flight** pass then scans the generated LaTeX and refuses to invoke the engine if a bare `$` survives inside a `\[ … \]` or if `\[`/`\]` do not balance, naming the nearest preceding heading for each fault so it is findable in the canon. **(f) Build hygiene.** `aux_is_corrupt` validates each auxiliary file (it must end on a line boundary and its braces must balance), `clean_aux` removes the corrupt ones before compiling and again after any failed compile so a failure cannot poison the next run, `log_shows_aux_poison` recognises the truncated-`.aux` signature and says so in plain language instead of showing raw TeX, and `--clean` forces the removal. **(g) Typography.** `\section` now takes a short optional argument — everything before the first em dash, so a 163-character tier title runs as `Tier BM.22` — which fixes the running head *and* shortens a very long table of contents, while the full title still reaches the PDF bookmark through `\texorpdfstring`; `headheight` rose to 17pt with `headsep` 14pt, which retires the recurring `fancyhdr` warning that demanded 39.25pt, and the header is set in `\small`. Paragraph rhythm tightened (`\parskip 2pt plus 1pt`) and arrays given `\arraystretch 1.08`. **(h) Verification.** A 3000-line probe of the real preamble and front matter compiles three passes clean under `xelatex` — zero errors, zero `fancyhdr` warnings, the table of contents populated, 49 pages out — and a full-corpus `xelatex` run reached page 1209 with **zero** genuine errors and **zero** `headheight` warnings before it was stopped; the delivered PDF was restored rather than left as a partial build. The suite is unaffected and green: **77/77**, with `check_format.py` now reporting 337/808/1497/4559/313 math segments against 296/804/1497/4084/313 before, the rise being the displays that were previously invisible to the segment splitter. **(i) Note for NixOS.** `tectonic` from `--fetch-engine` works, or put `texlive` in the shell profile (`fontspec` needs Latin Modern); after any interrupted build, `--clean` clears the poisoned auxiliaries.

**(V.28) The row-break repair: one ambiguous `\\[` in the exponent census, and the four guards around it.** Five tooling-side and source-side items; the mathematics of no tag is altered, and the repaired site is a *delimiter*, not content. **(a) The failure.** The engine reached page 1216 and halted with `! Illegal unit of measure (pt inserted).` at `.tex` line 87141, inside BM.102(2)'s derived exponent census, on the row break between the `\rho,\ [T_{\mu\nu}]` row and the `[\kappa^2],\ [G_N],\ [\kappa]` row. **(b) The cause.** LaTeX reads `\\[6pt]` as a row break carrying an optional vertical space, so an `array` row whose first cell begins with a literal `[` makes TeX read that cell as a dimension: `\\[\kappa^2]` asks for `\kappa^2` in points. This is ill-formed LaTeX in any engine, and MathJax mis-parses the same construct in the screen reader — it is not a renderer artefact. **(c) The locus.** Exactly one site corpus-wide, Part 4 line 6560, because every other row of every other array in the canon begins with `\text{…}`, `\mathfrak{…}` or a letter; the exponent census is the only table with a bracketed quantity in first position, and it is the only row transition in that table written without an intervening `\hline`. **(d) The repair.** The row break takes the standard empty group, `\\{}[\kappa^2]`, which absorbs the optional-argument scan and leaves the cell as ordinary content; the rendered table is unchanged. Verified against `xelatex` on the array in isolation: the unrepaired form reproduces `! Illegal unit of measure (pt inserted).` exactly, and the repaired form compiles clean. **(e) The guards, four deep.** `repair_display` already braced array rows beginning with `[`, but its pattern required a newline between the row break and the bracket and this array is written on one line, so the existing defence did not reach it; the newline is optional, and the pass is verified on four forms — the markdown `\\\\[` and the plain-LaTeX `\\[` both become `\\{}[`, a genuine `\\[6pt]` is left alone, and an already-braced row is not doubled. The **pre-flight** refuses to invoke the engine on a `\\[` that is not a length, naming the nearest preceding heading. `check_format.py` carries the same detection at source level, with the fix stated in its message. And a latent defect in that checker's brace counter, exposed by the repair, is fixed: it stripped `\\[{}]` escapes before neutralising escaped backslashes, so a row break `\\\\` followed by a real group `{}` lost its `{` to the escape rule and a balanced segment was reported unbalanced — escaped backslashes are neutralised first, and the counter still reports genuinely unbalanced segments on all five forms tested. **(f) Verification of the unreached tail.** The regenerated 109136-line `.tex` carries zero ambiguous row breaks, its displays balance at 19779 `\[` against 19779 `\]` (the seven apparent extras being legitimate `\\[<length>]` row breaks), and its brace depth returns to 0 without going negative. The portion of the document no engine had reached — `.tex` line 87139 to the end, 22039 lines, 322 pages — was compiled under the real preamble with **zero** genuine errors and zero undefined references. The single `! `-prefixed line in that log is a continuation of an `Overfull \hbox` diagnostic whose typeset material contains `(n-1)! 1`; it is not an error. **(g) The overfull repair loop converges.** That loop had never executed on a full build, the engine having halted before it. Run end-to-end on the tail, 434 display and 3 paragraph overfulls fell to 218/1, then 30/1, then 0/1, then 0/0 across six rounds and the loop returned success, so the default budget is sufficient and `--max-repair-rounds` (default 8) covers it; the module docstring's "max 5 rounds" is corrected to match. **(h) Suite state: 77/77.**

**(V.29) The Tier BM.23 pass — the Closing Tier: the Baryomesonic era is closed.** The era canon gains Tier BM.23 in Part 4 (Def-BM.20 and BM.131–BM.144) and the ledger gains its era-final state. **(i) The Haar–Poisson duality and the lapse (BM.131–BM.132):** the exact K-update $\delta K=\gamma\rho+\log Z_\gamma I$ as a formal-series identity with $\delta K=0$ at the vacuum; the twirl theorem (the Haar average is the block-maximal-mixed projection on A3's $3\oplus1$, idempotent, equivariant on 40 exact rational SU(2) points); the unique common fixed point $\{\tfrac14I\}$; the complementary factorization (the unrecorded draws drop out of $V_n$ exactly); the finite-group companion (the $S_k$-twirl is the symmetrizer); and the proper-time contraction $V(s)=(1-\tfrac\gamma4)^{2s/N}V_0$ as a reparametrization, item 21(b) re-scoped. The lapse is derived as $N(k,E)=\sqrt{1-\frac{G}{4\pi k}}=\sqrt{1-r_s/r}$ with its formed domain $k\ge\frac G{4\pi}$, the envelope row (the self-lapse is $\neg$formed at the Born width on every computed cell, on the exact enclosure $e<\tfrac{876809}{322560}<4$), the interior row, the vacuum-gauge limit, and the three substitutions (dispersion, threshold, coupling). **(ii) The composition selected (BM.133):** $\mathsf g=\frac1{1+G}$ by Lyapunov contractivity ($|F'|=1-(1+G)\mathsf g_{\mathrm{fp}}\in(0,1)$ unconditionally, on the certificate $0<4a^2G^2$) and minimal lapse-domain loss (the strict inflation chain); BM.104(4)'s loop exclusion untouched; the running coupling complete with only $e_0$ left to calibration. **(iii) The resolvent and the phase space (Def-BM.20, BM.134):** item 17 closed — the form-uniqueness theorem pins the resolvent on five derived conditions; $d\mu_{\mathrm{shell}}=d^3p/(2E)$; $\Phi_2=\frac{\sqrt{z^2-4N^2m^2}}{8\pi N^3z}$ and both fluxes exact; the two-resolvent species separation. **(iv) The censuses (BM.135–BM.136):** Q10 closed and deficit 10 closed (twelve $|Q|=2$ states at $k=3$, two at $k=2$, two stretched $|Q|=3$); the read gauged; the $S_3$-colour equivariance and the intertwiner theorem; **(R7$'$) closed** with $\mathcal H_n$, the 576-pair product law at $n=4$, $\chi(\sigma)=d^{c(\sigma)}$ on all 24 permutations at $d\in\{2,3\}$, the 625-quadruple exchange law (312/313) and $\dim\operatorname{Inv}((\mathbb C^3)^{\otimes4})=0$ by the weight census; **(V.30) The Tier BM.24 pass — the Continuum-Limit Tier: the foundational era is closed.** The era canon gains Tier BM.24 in Part 4 (BM.145–BM.152), and Parts 1–2 gain two in-place amendments. **(i) The force operator over the graph (BM.145):** the exact block census $3+3+5+3+1+1=16=C_{\mathrm{cap}}$, the mask ladder $(6,16,17,25)$ with its derived increments, and the eight-step composed action from registrations to $(M,g_{\mu\nu},T_{\mu\nu},\operatorname{spec})$ — every step a derived map, no step a datum; the state-side Haar/tilt face separated by species and bound-preserving; the locked face $O(n)=\max(0,2^{n+1}-16)$ exact to $n=8$. **(ii) The continuum-limit function (BM.146):** $\mathcal C(E,r)$ in five closed forms with the four exact domain conditions — the capacity condition $E\le2r/\mathfrak h$ among them — and the derived $\mathfrak h\to0$ row: the capacity outruns every finite content while the edges-per-cell grow without bound; the notation row keeps $\mathfrak q$ as the bookkeeper and $S_{\mathfrak L}$ as the flux. **(iii) The metric–energy–continuum map (BM.147):** the dictionary bijection; the closed-form dust solve whose linearized self-field is a pure-trace conformal perturbation, $g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{\mathfrak hE}{2r}\mathbb 1_4+O(\cdot)$, with the energy–lapse identity $N^2=\Omega^2=1-\frac{\mathfrak hE}{2r}$; the on-shell inverse $m=\frac1{\kappa^2}\int R\,dV$; the continuum as the CT2.6 quotient target. **(iv) The threshold (BM.148):** $E_{\mathrm{crit}}(r)=2r/\mathfrak h$, $S_{\mathrm{crit}}=2r\ln2/\mathfrak h$, $k_{\mathrm{crit}}=E^2\ln2/2$ with the dimension census; the one-hop capacity is exactly the digon floor; $r_s=(1,3,6,12,30)\mathfrak h$ on the computed cells; the envelope corollary by a second counting route ($E^2\ln2\le2$ has no integer solution $\ge2$ on $16>e^2$). **(v) The locked-sector complement (BM.149):** the two faces of one capacity; the anchors $(448,896,2976,2240,24384)$ re-derived; the membrane, the retention rows and the entropy-triangle vertex re-cited; the dynamic emission scoped as a ledger move; **the complement theorem — what the metric cannot read, the ledger still retains.** **(vi) The universality (BM.150):** one operator, one arena, one Lovelock-unique action, three fingerprints; the seven-row per-force table; the source-free pp-wave witness; the class-degenerate contraction. **(vii) The reclassification (BM.151) — the corpus's last non-axiomatic import is dissolved:** CT2.6(ii) is witnessed by the ledger's own unbounded ladder (constructive cycle witnesses $L=2..8$; **(V.9) The render-tooling repair and the screen reader.** Three renderer-side items; no canon text is touched. (a) **Repair:** `render_paper.py`'s table font ladder raised `re.error: bad escape \s` — the replacement template of a `re.sub` was built as `"{" + newsize`, so a size command such as `\small` entered the template as the invalid escape `\s`. The rung is now applied through a function replacement (the first tier to emit a table wide enough to reach that ladder exposed it). The table ladder also gains its last rung: at level 3 the column separation drops to `2pt` with `\LTleft/\LTright` zeroed, instead of dead-ending. (b) **Breakable table cells:** inline math inside `p`-columns now receives `\allowbreak` at top-level relations and operators (brace-depth tracked, so no macro argument or `\text{...}` is split), and the measured-cell metrics count the new break opportunities — the longest unbreakable chunk of a math-heavy cell drops (e.g. 22 to 16 visible chars), so wide tables hold a larger font instead of shrinking to `\scriptsize`. (c) **Two reading builds:** `render_paper.py --wide` (landscape 11x8.5in, 0.7in margins, ~9.6in text block; the display-breaking threshold rises from 80 to 118 visible chars and the table font caps rescale — measured on the current canon: 47177 lines of LaTeX at the default width against 34270 at `--wide`, i.e. roughly a quarter fewer split displays), writing `paper/qubitick_corpus_wide.{tex,pdf}`; and the new `render_html.py`, which writes a single self-contained `paper/qubitick_reader.html` (385 tag cards with anchors, a filterable tag index, foldable proofs, scrolling displays and tables, MathJax typesetting, dark-mode and print stylesheets). The HTML reader is a reading artifact only: the `$$`-delimiter tokenizer handles the corpus's mid-line display openings, and no mathematics is re-flowed, split or resized in it. Suite state unchanged: 60/60.

**(V.31) Revision 1 — the signature de-axiomatization and the open-ledger retirement.** Two author-directed revisions applied to the era canon; byte-identical copies of the pre-revision corpus are frozen in the publication repository's `working_markdowns/` archive. **(i) The signature pass ($\eta$ de-axiomatization).** A3 no longer names the vector $\eta=(-,+,+,+)$: it states the structural fact alone — $\eta\in\{\pm1\}^4$ with exactly one odd entry, $\exists!\,\mathfrak o:\ \eta^{\mathfrak o}=-\eta^{\mu}\ \forall\mu\ne\mathfrak o$. Convention C13 (Part 1 §B) declares the representative — the odd slot $\mathfrak o:=0$ (the identity slot), $\eta^{0}:=-1$, i.e. $\eta:=(-,+,+,+)$ (mostly-plus, matching D2.1) — and records the freedom boundary openly: the overall sign does not change $T$; the three spatial odd-slot choices are conjugate under $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ relabelling; $\mathfrak o=0$ versus $\mathfrak o\ge1$ is the choice of the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$-covariant representative, on which CT1.2's generator table rests. The new theorem CT1.0 (Tier 1A) classifies the $T$-grading: the uniqueness of $(\mathfrak o,\text{sign})$; $T[\eta]=T[-\eta]$ with $T=\mathsf D_\zeta\otimes\mathsf D_\zeta$; $[S,T]=0$ and the sector dimensions $(7,3,3,3)$ at every $\mathfrak o$; $\mathfrak o=0\iff\mathsf D_\zeta=\mathrm{sf}$ (the spin flip), the conjugacy classes of the four gradings, and the covariance characterization $T\,\mathrm{Ad}_{U\otimes U}=\mathrm{Ad}_{U\otimes U}\,T\ \forall U\in\mathrm{SU}(2)\iff\mathfrak o=0$; the Rmk censuses what $\mathfrak o\ge1$ would do to CT1.2's table (the selection is load-bearing, not cosmetic). Its content checker is `checks/verify_CT10_signature.py` (§A–§G; exact arithmetic only: exhaustive sign-vector enumeration over $\{\pm1\}^4$; exact $\mathbb Q(i)$ Pauli and tensor computations; the cyclic conjugation by the exact rational unitary $U_c=\tfrac12(\mathbb1-i(\sigma_1+\sigma_2+\sigma_3))$; the charpoly witnesses $(x+1)^3$ vs $(x-1)^2(x+1)$ for the non-conjugacy; the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$-covariance as polynomial identities over $\mathbb Q(i)[a,b,c,d]$ on the generic unitary form; the CT1.2 17-generator sector census at all four $\mathfrak o$; document pins). Downstream: CT1.1's and CT1.2's Req cite C13 and CT1.0 and their Pf justification lines carry [C13] at the representative uses; CT1.11a's involution table and CT1.11b's native-frames clause carry the representative tag; L5 renames the generator $T$-eigenvalue product $\eta_a\eta_b$ to $\vartheta_a\vartheta_b$ (off the slot vector); §D gains the $(\mathfrak o,\eta^{(\mathfrak o)})$ and $(\zeta,\mathsf D_\zeta,\mathrm{sf})$ rows and updates the $S,T$ and $\eta$ rows; the representative citations are swept in Part 3 (FT.156, FT.158), Part 4 (BM.9, BM.19, BM.152's Req), this ledger's §III FT.156 row, and the root README's range rows (C1–C13; CT1.0–CT1.11b). Option B (deriving $\mathfrak o=0$ from a covariance clause inside A3) stands proved at CT1.0(5) but is not adopted: the selection is stated openly in C13 instead, keeping the axiom surface minimal. **(ii) The open-ledger retirement.** The open-item apparatus (Open Gaps items 1–21, Q1–Q12, the deficits) is declared by the author to be Standard-Model-comparison-era residue: no Req edge ever resolved to a ledger item (the citation graph is closed over tags alone, ledger citations being pointers $\neg$premises), and the base layer stands self-contained — what remains is application, separate from the base layer's internal consistency. 283 ledger pointers are removed mechanically from Parts 2–4 and the Census (bracketed and parenthetical locators, Req-line segments, and comma-glued locator tails); tag citations glued to locators (the FT.140 `(HistLog §IV)` pattern), [math]/[machine] justifications, HistLog §I declaration-record locators, and every clause's mathematics are untouched; the five canon documents keep their line counts, $$ balance and table-cell structure. Content-level open-status language — clauses whose deliverable IS an open status — is inventoried for authorial ruling in the publication repository at `source/OPEN_ITEMS_REPORT.md` (325 lines in main-corpus blocks, 127 in the addendum-bound adjudication records) and is not silently rewritten. This ledger's §II stands as the historical record. **(iii) The suite and the registry.** `check_format.py` and `verify_corpus_graph.py` register C13 (the convention range moves to C1–C13); `verify_H_3p1_chiral_asymmetry.py`'s A3 literal pin is replaced by the revised-A3 and C13 pins; three pointer pins (`verify_H_topo_blind_neutron.py` §G, `verify_H_noether.py` §F, `verify_H_neutron_decay_vertex.py` §D12) are re-expressed against the revised canon; the suite-count pins move 79→80 across the checkers and both READMEs. Registry: 623 tags, 8012 Req edges, acyclic; the index (`index/`) and the Summary of Record (`Summary.md`) are regenerated. **Suite state: 80/80.**

**(V.32) The mass-nomenclature reconciliation (authorial ruling).** The mass reads of the corpus are reconciled to a single operative concept. **(i) The naming:** Def-H.39's registry read (3) — the inertial/tilt read $m_i^2(q)=\Delta S(q)$ — is named **the Lyapunov-contraction read**: the deficit accumulated under the L1 contraction law (every tilt step loses $\gamma(8-\gamma)/16$ of the Lyapunov value). **(ii) The ruling, recorded at BM.97 (Rmk):** the mass that works — the read entering every formed composite quantity (the rest masses, the Q-values, the splittings, the thresholds, the lapse) — is the Lyapunov-contraction mass, in its composite form $m_{\mathrm{rest}}=\Delta S_{\mathfrak L}/\mathfrak h$ [BM.35], with the class-degenerate foundation of BM.51; the informational read agrees with it as a formed equation [BM.35], $\neg$as a currency fusion; the pole read is the rest mass on the transport species, matched at leading order [BM.97(1)]; the sector-route values are measure functionals — exact named prices, $\neg$masses [BM.30; BM.37]; the Ansatz-B class masses remain non-adopted templates [Def-H.28]; the numeric bridges (the $\beta$-coefficients; $\rho_{\mathrm B}$) are calibration slots, values $\neg$issued [§Cal-BM (C-BM.1); P5]. **(iii) The nomenclature rule (Def-H.39):** every occurrence of *mass* names one registry read at the species declared at its locus; no unqualified *mass* crosses species; a computed variant value is a *price* — an exact named functional of the declaring data — and $\neg$ a mass read. **(iv)** BM.51's deficit species carries the registry qualification [Def-H.39(3)]. No mathematical clause is altered by this entry: the ruling records which derived read is operative and fixes the vocabulary; all citations respect the era chain. Suite state unchanged: 80/80.
